**Phase A — All 6 items done. Migration 023 live. Deployed.**

| Item | What it does |
|---|---|
| **A1 Opt-Out DB** | Inbound negative reply now stamps `opted_out_at` + `opt_out_reason` on the lead record — full audit trail |
| **A2 Reply Clears Follow-Up** | Inbound positive reply automatically NULLs `follow_up_date` — no more auto-SMS firing on a lead who already responded |
| **A3 DNC Safeguard** | `followup_queue()` auto-fire now filters `opted_out_at IS NULL` — opted-out leads can never get an auto-SMS |
| **A4 Stale Lead Alert** | `followup_queue()` now surfaces a second Slack alert for leads 14+ days overdue, with or without contact ID |
| **A5 Decision Outcome** | New command: `decision outcome <id> result:"What happened"` — stamps outc

🤖 CLAUDE:
**Decision bug root cause:** `split(None, 2)` left `"fe14171a go deeper first..."` as a single string. The `WHERE id::text LIKE 'fe14171a go deeper..%'` matched nothing. Fixed by trimming `did` to its first whitespace-delimited token before returning. Works for both approve and decline, and handles notes/reasons typed after the ID.

| **B4 KPI Trends** | Daily snapshot table live. `scorecard trends` shows 📈📉 week-over-week delta per KPI |
| **B5 Revenue MoM** | `revenue` now shows May: $X \

👤 USER:
What is A/B b;ast variant tracking? Any other suggestions to make Odin more like APEX?

---

🤖 CLAUDE:
**A/B blast variant tracking** — when you send an SMS blast to sellers, you'd have two message variants (A and B) sent to split groups. ODIN would track which variant each lead received, then compare response rates. After 48 hours: "Variant A got 12% reply rate, Variant B got 8%." Tells you which message copy works better. Currently ODIN sends blasts but treats all messages identically with no split tracking.

---

| Gap | What It Would Do |
|---|---|

| **Telegram** | Already built (`telegram_notify.py`) — needs `TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN` + `TELEGRAM_BROCK_

What is A/B b;ast variant tracking? Any other suggestions to make Odin more like APEX?

---

🤖 CLAUDE:
**A/B blast variant tracking** — when you send an SMS blast to sellers, you'd have two message variants (A and B) sent to split groups. ODIN would track which variant each lead received, then compare response rates. After 48 hours: "Variant A got 12% reply rate, Variant B got 8%." Tells you which message copy works better. Currently ODIN sends blasts but treats all messages identically with no split tracking.

## Voice Briefing (Twilio) — Ready to Go Live

Add these 5 vars in **Railway → Variables**:

| Variable | Value |
|---|---|
| `TWILIO_ACCOUNT_SID` | From twilio.com/console |
| `TWILIO_AUTH_TOKEN` | From twilio.com/console |
| `TWILIO_FROM_NUMBER` | A Twilio number you own, e.g. `+19015551234` |
| `BROCK_PHONE_NUMBER` | Your cell, e.g. `+19015559876` |
| `VOICE_BRIEFING_ENABLED` | `true` |

Then from Slack: `call me` or `brief me` — ODIN will phone you and read the morning briefing aloud using Amazon Polly (Matthew voice, US English).

Cost: ~$1.15/mo for number + $0.013/min per call. Pennies.

---

## Voice Briefing (Twilio) — Ready to Go Live

Add these 5 vars in **Railway → Variables**:

| Variable | Value |
|---|---|
| `TWILIO_ACCOUNT_SID` | From twilio.com/console |
| `TWILIO_AUTH_TOKEN` | From twilio.com/console |
| `TWILIO_FROM_NUMBER` | A Twilio number you own, e.g. `+19015551234` |
| `BROCK_PHONE_NUMBER` | Your cell, e.g. `+19015559876` |
| `VOICE_BRIEFING_ENABLED` | `true` |

Then from Slack: `call me` or `brief me` — ODIN will phone you and read the morning briefing aloud using Amazon Polly (Matthew voice, US English).

Cost: ~$1.15/mo for number + $0.013/min per call. Pennies.

🤖 CLAUDE:
Good news — `py` works and the browser opened. The 400 error means Google rejected the OAuth request because the **Gmail API scopes aren't enabled** in your Google Cloud Console project yet. The Calendar was set up with only the Calendar scope, but now we're adding Gmail scopes and Google is rejecting the expanded request.

**Fix — takes 3 minutes in Google Cloud Console:**

1. Go to **console.cloud.google.com**
2. Select your **ODIN project** (top-left dropdown)
3. Left menu → **APIs & Services → Library**
4. Search `Gmail API` → click it → click **Enable**
5. Left menu → **APIs & Services → OAuth consent screen**
6. Scroll to **Scopes** → click **Add or Remove Scopes**
7. Add these two scopes:
   - `https://www.googleapis.com/auth/gmail.send`
   - `https://www.googleapis.com/auth/gmail.r

```

## Voice Briefing (Twilio) — Ready to Go Live

Add these 5 vars in **Railway → Variables**:

| Variable | Value |
|---|---|
| `TWILIO_ACCOUNT_SID` | From twilio.com/console |
| `TWILIO_AUTH_TOKEN` | From twilio.com/console |
| `TWILIO_FROM_NUMBER` | A Twilio number you own, e.g. `+19015551234` |
| `BROCK_PHONE_NUMBER` | Your cell, e.g. `+19015559876` |
| `VOICE_BRIEFING_ENABLED` | `true` |

Then from Slack: `call me` or `brief me` — ODIN will phone you and read the morning briefing aloud using Amazon Polly (Matthew voice, US English).

Cost: ~$1.15/mo for number + $0.013/min per call. Pennies.

🤖 CLAUDE:
Good news — `py` works and the browser opened. The 400 error means Google rejected the OAuth request because the **Gmail API scopes aren't enabled** in your Google Cloud Console project yet. The Calendar was set up with only the Calendar scope, but now we're adding Gmail scopes and Google is rejecting the expanded request.

**Fix — takes 3 minutes in Google Cloud Console:**

1. Go to **console.cloud.google.com**
2. Select your **ODIN project** (top-left dropdown)
3. Left menu → **APIs & Services → Library**
4. Search `Gmail API` → click it → click **Enable**
5. Left menu → **APIs & Services → OAuth consent screen**
6. Scroll to **Scopes** → click **Add or Remove Scopes**
7. Add these two scopes:
   - `https://www.googleapis.com/auth/gmail.send`
   - `https://www.googleapis.com/auth/gmail.r

## OpenClaude = openclaw (GitHub: `openclaw/openclaw`)

**376,000+ stars. 78,400+ forks.** One of the largest open-source AI projects alive. This is what APEX is built on top of. Dan customized it for security and simplicity.

What openclaw actually does that's relevant to ODIN:

| Capability | Details |
|---|---|
| **Multi-channel** | WhatsApp, Telegram, Slack, Discord, Signal, iMessage (BlueBubbles), Teams, Matrix, LINE, IRC — all native |

🤖 CLAUDE:
---

**Gumloop — Confirmed pricing:**

| Plan | Cost | Credits | Best For |
|---|---|---|---|
| Free | $0/mo | 2,000 credits | Testing |
| Solo | $37/mo | 10,000 credits | One person |
| Team | $244/mo | 60,000 credits | Small team |
| Enterprise | Custom | Custom | Large orgs |

Credit consumption: standard AI calls = 2 credits, Claude Sonnet / GPT-4 = 20 credits. At Solo ($37/mo), 10,000 credits ÷ 20 = 500 Claude calls/month — plenty for most automations.

**Verdict on Gumloop:** $37/mo Solo plan is genuinely cheap for what it replaces. Worth it if you want to offload any ODIN heartbeat jobs to no-code flows without touching Python.

---

**Revio — No public pricing.** It's a "book a call" model, which in this space almost always means $300–$700+/mo. Targets creators with large Instagram

🤖 CLAUDE:
Two things to flag here:

**1. ODIN shows 0% reply rate — XLeads shows actual replies. Webhook mismatch.**

ODIN's blast stats pull from the database, which only gets populated when replies come through the inbound webhook. Those two replies ("No" and "Wrong number") exist in XLeads but ODIN never received the webhook event. Either the webhook isn't firing for replies to this workflow, or the `blast_campaign_id` isn't being linked when the reply comes in. The reply_scanner heartbeat should be catching this — it's not.

**2. The workflow re-messaged Kenyata after she already said no.**

She said "No m,ma" on May 29. The next day, the workflow fired again: *"Hey Kenyata, not sure if this is the right number..."* — that's a second touch after a negative reply. The workflow has no negative rep

| Plan | Cost | Credits | Best For |
|---|---|---|---|
| Free | $0/mo | 2,000 credits | Testing |
| Solo | $37/mo | 10,000 credits | One person |
| Team | $244/mo | 60,000 credits | Small team |
| Enterprise | Custom | Custom | Large orgs |

Credit consumption: standard AI calls = 2 credits, Claude Sonnet / GPT-4 = 20 credits. At Solo ($37/mo), 10,000 credits ÷ 20 = 500 Claude calls/month — plenty for most automations.

**Verdict on Gumloop:** $37/mo Solo plan is genuinely cheap for what it replaces. Worth it if you want to offload any ODIN heartbeat jobs to no-code flows without touching Python.

🤖 CLAUDE:
Two things to flag here:

**1. ODIN shows 0% reply rate — XLeads shows actual replies. Webhook mismatch.**

ODIN's blast stats pull from the database, which only gets populated when replies come through the inbound webhook. Those two replies ("No" and "Wrong number") exist in XLeads but ODIN never received the webhook event. Either the webhook isn't firing for replies to this workflow, or the `blast_campaign_id` isn't being linked when the reply comes in. The reply_scanner heartbeat should be catching this — it's not.

1. XLeads → Automation → find `customer-replied` workflow (ID ends in `9184a227...`)

3. Set:
   - URL: `https://odin-api-production-e85d.up.railway.app/api/xleads/inbound`
   - Method: POST
   - Content-Type: `application/json`
4. Payload (map the GHL merge fields):
```json
{

  "direction": "inbound",
  "contactId": "{{contact.id}}",
  "firstName": "{{contact.f

**Bad news:** Every single webhook call is **failing** — "Failed" → retry → retry → "Failed Retry Limit Reached." ODIN is returning a non-200 response (likely a 500 crash).

The trigger works, the URL is right — ODIN is crashing when it receives the payload.

**Check Railway logs right now** — go to Railway → ODIN service → Logs, and look for entries around these timestamps (all May 31st):
- 7:17 AM, 7:22 AM, 7:32 AM (Daandrea Kelly)
- 7:44 AM, 7:54 AM, 8:09 AM (Crisofloro Duarte)

You should see a Python traceback near those times. Paste it here and I'll fix it.

**Most likely cause:** GHL's standard "Customer Replied" payload uses different field

---

👤 USER:
Check, diagnose, fic it

---

🤖 CLAUDE:
Found it. The crash is on line 5869:

```python
body = (data.get('body') or data.get('message') or '').strip()
```

GHL's "Customer Replied" webhook sends `message` as a **nested dict** (`{"body": "...", "type": "SMS", "direction": "inbound"}`), not a string. When ODIN calls `.strip()` on a dict → `AttributeError: 'dict' object has no attribute 'strip'` → 500 → GHL retries → fails.

Also means `direction` and `type` are inside that nested object and never extracted. Two bugs in one line.

---

🤖 CLAUDE:
Now deploy it:

---

🤖 CLAUDE:
Deployed. Railway will rebuild in ~2 min.

**What was wrong:** GHL sends the reply like this:
```json
{ "message": { "body": "No m,ma", "direction": "inbound", "type": "SMS" }, "contactId": "..." }
```

ODIN tried to call `.strip()` on that dict → crashed with 500 on every single reply → GHL retried 3–4 times → "Failed Retry Limit Reached." All 33 replies hit this same crash.

**After deploy:** The next reply from any contact will go through cleanly — body extracted from the nested object, direction detected automatically, no custom data fields needed in XLeads. Watch Railway logs and Slack for the first live hit.

👤 USER:
Describe A/B blast tracking to me simply, confused by that

---

🤖 CLAUDE:
Right now when you fire a blast, every contact gets the **same SMS message**.

A/B tracking means you split the list — half gets **Message A**, half gets **Message B** — and ODIN tracks which one got more replies.

Example:
- **Message A:** "Hey [name], inherited your property at [address]? We buy as-is, cash. Interested?"
- **Message B:** "Hi [name], I saw your property on [address] — any chance you'd consider a cash offer?"

After a week you run `blast stats` and ODIN tells you: Message A got 8% reply rate, Message B got 3%. Now you know which copy works and you use that for every blast going forward.

**Why it matters for you specifically:** Your last blast had 68% failure rate (landlines) and 7.14% opt-out. Once you fix the skip-trace filter, you'll be blasting a much cleaner list — an

## Why Telegram Was Introduced

The APEX feature gap was **multi-channel** — Dan's system handles Slack + Email + WhatsApp + social DMs in one unified feed. ODIN only had Slack + Email (just activated).

WhatsApp was the obvious target, but it requires **Meta Business Verification** — a registration process that takes days to weeks and requires a business phone number approved by Meta. Not worth it to unblock one feature.

Telegram is functionally identical for this use case:
- Bot API works the same way (webhook → parse command → reply)
- No verification, instant setup via BotFather
- Same practical result: you can text your ODIN bot from your phone and get alerts outside of Slack

It's a pragmatic substitute, not a permanent soluti

## Why Telegram Was Introduced

The APEX feature gap was **multi-channel** — Dan's system handles Slack + Email + WhatsApp + social DMs in one unified feed. ODIN only had Slack + Email (just activated).

WhatsApp was the obvious target, but it requires **Meta Business Verification** — a registration process that takes days to weeks and requires a business phone number approved by Meta. Not worth it to unblock one feature.

Telegram is functionally identical for this use case:
- Bot API works the same way (webhook → parse command → reply)
- No verification, instant setup via BotFather
- Same practical result: you can text your ODIN bot from your phone and get alerts outside of Slack

It's a pragmatic substitute, not a permanent soluti

3. It replies with your user ID — a number like `123456789`

What

---

👤 USER:
How do I create a gmail alias

---

👤 USER:
how do I create a Gmail alias

---

🤖 CLAUDE:
Two options depending on what you want:

**Option A — Gmail "Send As" alias (simplest, free)**
ODIN sends from a different address but it routes through your existing Gmail account.

1. Go to Gmail → Settings (gear) → **See all settings**
2. **Accounts and Import** tab → "Send mail as" → **Add another email address**
3. Enter the alias (e.g. `odin@shueboxllc.com` or a new Gmail like `odin.shueboxllc@gmail.com`)
4. Gmail will send a verification code to that address — confirm it
5. Now when composing, you can pick which "From" address to use

**Option B — New Gmail account (cleanest isolation)**
Dedicated `odin.shueboxllc@gmail.com` account. ODIN gets its own inbox, sent folder, token.

1. Create the account normally at accounts.google.com
2. Run `gmail_client.py --setup` authenticated as t

---

👤 USER:
skip alias til next session . build and verify app py modularization, and dynamic sub-agent spawning, take your time and build them right the first time

What

👤 USER:
How do I create a gmail alias

---

👤 USER:
how do I create a Gmail alias

---

🤖 CLAUDE:
Two options depending on what you want:

**Option A — Gmail "Send As" alias (simplest, free)**
ODIN sends from a different address but it routes through your existing Gmail account.

1. Go to Gmail → Settings (gear) → **See all settings**
2. **Accounts and Import** tab → "Send mail as" → **Add another email address**
3. Enter the alias (e.g. `odin@shueboxllc.com` or a new Gmail like `odin.shueboxllc@gmail.com`)
4. Gmail will send a verification code to that address — confirm it
5. Now when composing, you can pick which "From" address to use

**Option B — New Gmail account (cleanest isolation)**
Dedicated `odin.shueboxllc@gmail.com` account. ODIN gets its own inbox, sent folder, token.

1. Create the account normally at accounts.google.com
2. Run `gmail_client.py --setup` authenticated as t

👤 USER:
How do I create a gmail alias

---

👤 USER:
how do I create a Gmail alias

---

🤖 CLAUDE:
Two options depending on what you want:

**Option A — Gmail "Send As" alias (simplest, free)**
ODIN sends from a different address but it routes through your existing Gmail account.

1. Go to Gmail → Settings (gear) → **See all settings**
2. **Accounts and Import** tab → "Send mail as" → **Add another email address**
3. Enter the alias (e.g. `odin@shueboxllc.com` or a new Gmail like `odin.shueboxllc@gmail.com`)
4. Gmail will send a verification code to that address — confirm it
5. Now when composing, you can pick which "From" address to use

**Option B — New Gmail account (cleanest isolation)**
Dedicated `odin.shueboxllc@gmail.com` account. ODIN gets its own inbox, sent folder, token.

1. Create the account normally at accounts.google.com
2. Run `gmail_client.py --setup` authenticated as t
